 9. What will be the value of the variable x, after executing the following Python statement?
    my; olajd we;s mhs;ka j.ka;sh l%shd;aul jQ miq x úp,Hfha w.h l=ula fõ o?
    x = 3 – 4 * 6 / 3 + 12 / 4 * 3
    1) –5.0                     2) – 4.0                 3) –1.0             4) 4.0          5) 5.0

                                                                                             ( 2016 - 42 )
    Answer: 4)
    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    When solving problems involving a large number of calculations, Python uses a mathematically driven
    precedence order. This order has remained unchanged from Python 2 to the current version of Python and
    is as follows:
    fujeks .Kkh lsÍï jeä .eg¨ úi§fï§ mhs;ka yS .KS;uh fufyhjk m%uqL;d wkqms<sfj,la Ndú;d
    lrkq ,nhs' th mhs;ka 2 isg oekg Ndú;d lrkq ,nk mhs;ka olajd fkdfjkiaj mej;=kq w;r tu
    .KS;uh fufyhjk m%uqL;d wkqms<sfj, my; mßÈ fõ'

                                Description                             Operator
        Parentheses                                                          ()
        Positive / Negative / Bitwise NOT                                 +, -, ~
        Exponentiation                                                      **
        Multiplication / Division / Integer / Modulus Division          *, /, //, %
        Addition / Subtraction                                              +, -
        Left Shift / Right Shift                                          <<, >>
        Bitwise AND                                                          &
        Bitwise XOR                                                          ^
        Bitwise OR                                                            |
        Comparisons                                                <, <=, >, >=, !=, ==
        Identity Operators                                         in, not in, is, is not
        Boolean NOT                                                         not
        Boolean AND                                                         and
        Boolean OR                                                           or

    The given expression is /§ we;s m%ldYkh jkafka : x = 3 – 4 * 6 / 3 + 12 / 4 * 3

    Resolve the multiplication and division from left to right
    jfï isg ol=Kg .=K lsÍu iy fn§u úi|kak

    •     4 * 6 = 24
    •     24 / 3 = 8
    •     12 / 4 = 3
    •     3*3=9
    Substitute the results into the expression
    m%;sM, m%ldYkhg wdfoaY lrkak

    •     x=3–8+9
    Perform addition and subtraction from left to right
    jfï isg ol=Kg tl;= lsÍu iy wvq lsÍu isÿ lrkak

    •     3 – 8 = -5
    •     -5 + 9 = 4
    So, the value of `x` is 4.0, and the correct answer is (4).
    tneúka" `x` ys w.h 4.0 jk w;r ksjerÈ ms<s;=r (4) fõ'

          3)    It has no process box / tys ‘process’ fldgqjla fkdue;s ùu

          termination paths.

 2. Which of the following statements is true about algorithms?
    we,af.dß;u iïnkaOfhka my; oelafjk l=uk j.ka;sh i;H fõ o?

    1) A set of steps used to solve a problem is called an algorithm.
       .eg¨jla úi£ug fhdod .kakd mshjrj, iuQyhla we,af.dß;uhla f,i ye¢kafõ'
    2) A sequence of activities used to solve a problem is called an algorithm.
       .eg¨jla úi£ug fhdod .kakd ld¾hj, wkql%uhla we,af.dß;uhla f,i ye¢kafõ'
    3) To solve any problem, there can be at most one algorithm.
       ´kEu .eg¨jla úi£u i|yd jeäukla u ;sìh yelafla tla we,af.dß;uhla muKs'
    4) An algorithm can contain an infinite number of steps.
       we,af.dß;uhlg wmßñ; mshjr ixLHdjla ;sìh yels h'
    5) An algorithm does not need to terminate within a finite time.
       we,af.dß;uhla mßñ; ld,hla ;=< § wjika ùug wjYH fkdfõ'

                                                                                                   ( 2017 - 29 )
    Answer: 2)

    Option / úl,amh 1)
    This is the correct definition of an algorithm. An algorithm is a finite, well-defined sequence of steps or
    instructions designed to solve a specific problem or perform a computation. It is precise and must
    produce an output after a finite number of steps.
    fuh we,af.dß;uhl ksjerÈ w¾: oelaùuhs' we,af.dß;uhla hkq ksYaÑ; .eg¿jla úi£ug fyda
    .Kkh lsÍula isÿ lsÍug ks¾udKh lr we;s iSñ;" fyd¢ka w¾: olajd we;s mshjr fyda Wmfoia
    wkqms<sfj,ls' th ksrjoH jk w;r iSñ; mshjr .Kklg miq m%;sodkhla ksmoúh hq;=h'

    Option / úl,amh 2)
    This is also correct. The term "sequence of activities" aligns with the idea of ordered steps in an
    algorithm, making this statement true as well.
    fuho ksjerÈh' "l%shdldrlï wkqms<sfj," hk moh we,af.dß;uhl wkqms<sfj,g ilia lrk ,o
    mshjr ms<sn| woyi iuÕ iumd; jk w;r" fuu m%ldYho i;H fõ'

    Option / úl,amh 3)
    This is false. Many problems can be solved using multiple algorithms. For example, sorting can be done
    using bubble sort, merge sort, quicksort, etc.
    fuh wi;Hhls' nyq we,af.dß;u Ndú;fhka fndfyda .eg¿ úi¢h yelsh' WodyrKhla f,i" bubble
    sort, merge sort, quicksort hkdÈh Ndú;fhka j¾. lsÍu l< yelsh'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                   -59-                                              #ictfromabc

    Option / úl,amh 4)
    This is incorrect. An algorithm must be finite - it must terminate after a known number of steps. Infinite
    steps would make it non-algorithmic.
    fuh jerÈhs' we,af.dß;uhla iSñ; úh hq;=h - th okakd mshjr .Kklg miqj wjika úh hq;=h'
    wkka; mshjr th we,af.dß;u fkdjk njg m;a lrhs'

    Option / úl,amh 5)
    This contradicts the basic definition of an algorithm. Termination is essential. otherwise, the algorithm is
    not considered valid or effective.
    fuh we,af.dß;uhl uQ,sl w¾: oelaùug mgyeks fõ' wjika lsÍu w;HjYHhs. tfia fkdue;s kï"
    we,af.dß;u j,x.= fyda M,odhS f,i fkdie,fla'

    Here, according to the Advanced Level answer sheet, answer 2 is correct, but answer 1 is also very
    correct. Therefore, both answers 1 and 2 are correct.
    fuys§ Wiia fm, ms<s;=re m;%hg wkqj 2 jk ms,s;=r ksjerÈ jk kuq;a 1 fjks ms<s;=ro b;du;a
    ksjerÈ fõ' tu ksid 1 iy 2 hk ms<s;=re folu ksjerÈ fõ'

    Answer - 1 and 2
    Therefore, option 1) and option 2) are the correct answers.

 3. Which of the following statements about Python data types/expressions is correct?
    Python o;a; m%rEm$m%ldYk ms<sno j my; oelafjk ljr j.ka;sh ksjerÈ fõ o?

    1) String is a mutable data type / ‘String’, hkq mutable o;a; m%rEmhls'
    2) List is an immutable data type / ‘List’ hkq immutable o;a; m%rEmhls'
    3) [1, 2, 3] is a tuple / [1, 2, 3] hkq tuple hls'
    4) The expression [1, 2, 3] [1] will produce [2] when executed / [1, 2, 3] [1] m%ldYkh l%shd;aul l<
       úg [2] ,nd foa'
    5) The type of {'a':l, 1:(1, 2)} is a Dictionary / {'a':1, 1:(1, 2)} m%rEmh Dictionary hls'

                                                                                                          ( 2017 - 41 )
    Answer: 5)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    In Python, a dictionary is a collection of key-value pairs where keys are unique and immutable types like
    strings, numbers, or tuples. The given expression {'a':1, 1:(1, 2)} satisfies this structure. Here, 'a' is a string,
    and 1 is an integer, both of which are valid keys, while their respective values, 1 and (1, 2), are of integer
    and tuple types, making it a valid dictionary.
    Python ys" dictionary hla hkq h;=re-w.h hq., tl;=jls" tys§ h;=re woaú;Sh iy fjkia l<
    fkdyels j¾. jk string" ixLHd fyda gm,a fõ' § we;s m%ldYkh {'a':1, 1:(1, 2)} fuu jHqyh ;Dma;su;a
    lrhs' fuys§" 'a' hkq string tlla jk w;r" 1 hkq mQ¾K ixLHdjla jk w;r" tA folu j,x.= h;=re
    jk w;r" tAjdfha wod< w.hka jk 1 iy (1,2) mQ¾K ixLHd iy gm,a j¾. jk w;r" th j,x.=
    dictionary hla njg m;a lrhs'

 5. Consider the following Python statement:

    1) 2, 4, 6       2) 3, 5           3) [2, 3]            4) [3, 5]           5) [2, 4, 6]

                                                                                                    ( 2017 - 43 )
    Answer: 4)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    The slicing operation [2::2] on the list [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6] starts from index 2 (third element, 3) and selects
    every second element thereafter. The step value of 2 ensures skipping one element between selections.
    Thus, the result is [3, 5], which includes only the elements at indices 2 and 4.
    [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6] ,ehsia;=fõ we;s slicing fufyhqu [2::2] o¾Ylh 2 ^f;jk uQ,øjHh" 3& isg wdrïN
    jk w;r bka miqj iEu fojk uQ,øjHhlau f;dard .kS' 2 ys mshjr w.h f;aÍï w;r tla uQ,øjHhla
    uÕ yeÍu iy;sl lrhs' fï wkqj" m%;sM,h [3, 5] jk w;r" thg o¾Yl 2 iy 4 ys uQ,øjH muKla
    we;=<;a fõ'
    Python slicing is a powerful tool for extracting sublists based on starting points, stopping points, and step
    sizes. In this case, the operation is straightforward and results in the expected sublist [3, 5] by following
    these slicing rules.
    mhs;ka yS slicing hkq wdrïNl ,laIH" kej;=ï ,laIH iy mshjr m%udKhka u; mokïj Wm ,ehsia;=
    Wmqgd .ekSu i|yd m%n, fuj,uls' fuu wjia:dfõ§" fufyhqu ir, jk w;r fuu slicing yS kS;s
    wkq.ukh lsÍfuka wfmalaIs; Wm ,ehsia;= [3, 5] ,nd fohs'
    Therefore, the correct answer is 4).

    = (2, 3), does the same using an explicit tuple. Finally, D assigns the tuple (2, 3) directly to a.

    gm,a (2, 3) iDcqju a fj; mjrhs'

 7. Consider the following Python expressions:

    Which of the above expressions would result in the Boolean value True?

    4) B and D only                     5) C and D only
       B iy D muKS                         C iy D muKs

    Answer: 2)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Option A evaluates 'True or False and True', which adheres to Python's operator precedence. The 'and'
    operator has higher precedence than 'or', so the expression simplifies to 'True or False', resulting in 'True'.
    Option C checks if two sets, '{2, 3}' and '{3, 2}', are equal. Since sets are unordered collections, the
    comparison evaluates to 'True'.
    úl,amh A uÕska 'True or False and True' hkak we.hSug ,la lrhs" th Python ys l%shdlre m%uqL;djhg
    wkq.; fõ' 'and' l%shdlreg 'or' g jvd by< m%uqL;djhla we;" tneúka m%ldYkh 'True or False' f,i
    ir, lrhs" tys m%;sM,hla f,i 'True' ,efí' C úl,amh uÕska '{2, 3}' iy '{3, 2}' hk lÜg, folla
    iudk oehs mÍlaId lrhs' lÜg, wkqms<sfj,g fkdmj;sk tl;= lsÍï neúka" ixikaokh 'True' f,i
    we.hSug ,la lrhs'

    Options B and D evaluate to 'False' due to logical and equality conditions failing. So, only A and C yield
    the Boolean value 'True', demonstrating the logical processing rules in Python.
    ;d¾lsl iy iudkd;au;d fldkafoais wid¾:l ùu fya;=fjka úl,am B iy D 'False' f,i we.hSug ,la
    lrhs' fï wkqj" A iy C muKla 'True' nQ,shka w.h ,nd fohs" th Python ys ;d¾lsl ieliqï kS;s
    fmkakqï lrhs'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 2).
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 2) fõ'

     (1)                                                        (2)
           f1=open("in.csv","r")                                      f1=open("in.csv","r")
           f2=open("out.csv","r")                                     f2=open("out.csv","w")
           for line in f1:                                            for line in f1:
             items=line.strip().split(",")                              items=line.strip()
             tot=int(items[1])+int(items[2])                            tot=int(items[1])+int(items[2])
             print(items[0],items[1],items[2],tot,file = f2)            print(items[0],items[1],items[2],tot)
           f1.close()                                                 f1.close()
           f2.close()                                                 f2.close()

     (3)                                                        (4)
           f1=open("in.csv","r")                                      f1=open("in.csv","r")

           f1.close()
           f2.close()

    using 'split(",")', and the numeric columns are converted to integers and added to calculate the total. This

   •      Questions 23 - 25 are based on the flowchart below. (Note that n%2 represents n mod 2.)
          wxl 23 isg 25 f;la m%Yak my; .e,Sï igyk wdY%fhks' (n%2 hkafkka n mod 2 oelafjk nj i,lkak')

                                   Start

                               i           1
                            result             0

                                  input n

             Yes
                            is n %2 = 0 ?

                                        No
                      result           result + n

                              i        i+1

                                                        Yes
                                  is i ≤ 10 ?

                                           No
                           output result

                                    Stop

   1. Which of the following is/are correct regarding the algorithm expressed by the above flowchart?
      by; .e,Sï igyk u.ska bÈßm;a flfrk we,af.daß;uh iïnkaOfhka my; ljrla ksjerÈ fõ o?
          A. It takes 10 inputs
             th wdodk 10 la .kshs'
          B. It computes the sum of the even numbers in the input.
             th wdodkfha we;s brÜfÜ ixLHdj, tl;=j .Kkh lrhs'
          C. To take 100 inputs, only modifying “is i ≤ 10?” will be sufficient.
             wdodk 100 la ,nd .ekSug “i ≤ 10 o?” hkak fjkia lsÍu muKla iEfya'

          1) A only.                    2) B only.                    3) C only.                    4) A and B only.                  5) A and C only.
             A muKs.                       B muKs.                       C muKs.                       A yd B muKs.                      A yd C muKs.

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2018 - 23 )

    Answer: 5)
    Understanding the Flowchart
    .e,Sï igyk f;areï .ekSu

    Initialization / wdrïN lsÍu
    i is set to 1.
    i 1 g ilid we;'
    result is set to 0.
    result 0 f,i ilid we;'

    Loop (Runs Until i > 10) / Æmh (i > 10 olajd l%shd;aul fõ&
    The algorithm takes an input n.
    we,af.dß;uh n f,i wdodkhla .kS'
    It checks if n % 2 = 0 (i.e., whether n is even).
    th n % 2 = 0 ^tkï" n brÜfÜ o hkak& mÍlaId lrhs'
    If yes, nothing happens.
    i;H kï" lsisjla isÿ fkdfõ'
    If no, n is added to result.
    tfia fkdfõ kï" result tlg n tl;= fõ'
    i is incremented by 1.
    i tllska jeä lr we;'
    The loop repeats until i exceeds 10.
    i w.h 10 blaujk ;=re Æmh mqkrdj¾;kh fõ'

    Termination / wjika lsÍu
    Once i > 10, it outputs result and stops.
    i > 10 jQ miq" th result m%;sodkh lr k;r fõ'

    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    The loop runs from i = 1 to i = 10, meaning it takes exactly 10 inputs.
    Æmh i = 1 isg i = 10 olajd Odjkh fõ" tkï thg yßhgu wdodk 10 la wjYH fõ'
    This statement is correct.
    fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈhs'

    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    The condition checks n % 2 = 0. If n is even, it is not added to result. Only odd numbers are added.
    fldkafoaish n % 2 = 0 mÍlaId lrhs' n brÜfÜ kï" th m%;sM,hg tl;= fkdfõ' T;af;a ixLHd muKla
    tl;= fõ'
    This statement is incorrect. The algorithm actually computes the sum of the odd numbers in the input.
    fuu m%ldYh jerÈhs' we,af.dß;uh we;a; jYfhkau wdodkfha we;s T;af;a ixLHd j, tl;=j .Kkh
    lrhs'

    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    The loop runs as long as i ≤ 10. If we change this condition to i ≤ 100, the loop will run 100 times.
    Æmh i ≤ 10 jk f;la l%shd;aul fõ' wms fuu ;;a;ajh i ≤ 100 f,i fjkia l<fyd;a" Æmh 100 j;djla
    l%shd;aul fõ'
    This means it will take 100 inputs instead of 10, which is exactly what the statement claims.
    fuhska woyia lrkafka thg wdodk 10la fjkqjg 100 la wjYH jkq we;s njhs" th m%ldYkfha i|yka
    jk wdldrhgu fõ'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                 -67-                                 #ictfromabc

 2. If the following were fed as inputs to the above algorithm, what will be the output?
    by; we,af.daß;uhg my; ixLHd wdodk f,i ,nd ÿkafka kï m%;sodkh l=ula fõ o?

    2, 8, 9, 3, 4, 10, 6, 5, 13, 19, 12, 7

    1) 10                 2) 30               3) 42              4) 49           5) 56

                                                                                           ( 2018 - 24 )
    Answer: 4)

    Understanding the Algorithm / we,af.drsouh f;areï .ekSu

    The algorithm follows these steps
    we,af.drsouh my; mshjr wkq.ukh lrhs

    It takes 10 inputs (since i starts at 1 and loops until i ≤ 10).
    thg wdodk 10 la wjYH fõ ^i 1 ka wdrïN ù i ≤ 10 olajd kej; kej; l%shd lrk neúka&'

    It only adds odd numbers to the result (numbers where n % 2 ≠ 0).
    th m%;sM,hg T;af;a ixLHd muKla tl;= lrhs ^n % 2 ≠ 0 we;s ixLHd&'
    It ignores even numbers (numbers where n % 2 = = 0).
    th brÜfÜ ixLHd fkdi,ld yßhs ^n % 2 = = 0 we;s ixLHd&'

    Processing the Given Inputs / § we;s wdodk ieliSu

    The given input sequence is
    ,nd § we;s wdodk wkqms<sfj,
    2, 8, 9, 3, 4, 10, 6, 5, 13, 19, 12, 7

    Since the loop runs for the first 10 numbers, we consider
    m<uq ixLHd 10 i|yd Æmh l%shd;aul jk neúka" wms i,ld n,kafka
    2, 8, 9, 3, 4, 10, 6, 5, 13, 19

    The classified numbers / j¾.SlrKh lrk ,o ixLHd

          Number                  Even/Odd?            Included in the result?
             2                      Even                      ignored
             8                      Even                      ignored
             9                       Odd                       added
             3                       Odd                       added
             4                      Even                      ignored
            10                      Even                      ignored
             6                      Even                      ignored
             5                       Odd                       added

 4. Consider the following statements regarding programming languages and program translation.
    l%uf,aL NdId iN l%uf,aL mßj¾;kh ms<sn| my; m%ldY i,lkak'
    A. A compiler translates a program one statement at a time.
       iïmdolhla (compiler) jrlg tla m%ldYh ne.ska" l%uf,aLhla mßj¾;kh lrhs'
    B. An assembler converts a program in an assembly language into machine code.
       tfiïí,rhla tfiïn,s NdIdfjka we;s l%uf,aLhla hka;% fla;hg (machine code) yrjhs'
    C. An interpreter scans the entire program and translates it as a whole into machine code.
       w¾: úkHdilhla (interpreter) uq¿ l%uf,aLhu mßf,dalkh (scan) lr th iuia;hla f,i hka;%
       fla;hg mßj¾;kh lrhs'
    Which of the above statements is/are correct?
    by; ljr m%ldYhla ksjerÈ fõ o?

    1) A only.          2) B only.           3) C only.          4) A and C only.       5) B and C only.
       A muKs.             B muKs.              C muKs.             A yd C muKs.           B iy C muKs.

                                                                                                     ( 2018 - 34 )
    Answer: 2)
    Machine code is the lowest level programming language that is directly understood by a computer's CPU.
    It consists of binary digits (0s and 1s). Low-level programming languages are closer to machine code but
    are easier for humans to read and write. The most common low-level language is Assembly Language.
    High-level programming languages are closer to human languages and are much easier to read, write, and
    maintain than low-level or machine code. Examples include Python, Java, C++, JavaScript.
    hka;% fla;h hkq mß.Klhl CPU u.ska iDcqju f;areï .; yels my<u uÜgfï l%uf,aLk NdIdjhs'
    th oaúuh b,lalï 0 iy 1 j,ska iukaú; fõ' my< uÜgfï l%uf,aLk NdId" hka;% fla;hg ióm
    kuq;a ñksiqkag lshùug iy ,sùug myiqh' jvd;a fmdÿ my< uÜgfï l%uf,aLk NdIdj jkafka tfiïí,s
    NdIdjhs' by< uÜgfï l%uf,aLk NdId ñksia NdIdj,g ióm jk w;r my< uÜgfï fyda hka;%
    fla;hg jvd lshùug" ,sùug iy kv;a;= lsÍug fnfyúka myiqh' WodyrK f,i Python, Java, C++,
    JavaScript we;=<;a fõ'
    Let's analyze each statement / wms tla tla m%ldYh úYaf,aIKh lruq
    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    This statement is incorrect. A compiler does not translate a program one statement at a time. It translates
    the entire program as a whole. It processes the entire source code, checking for errors, and then generates
    an executable file or machine code.
    fuu m%ldYh jerÈhs' iïmdolhla jrlg tla m%ldYhla ne.ska l%uf,aLhla mßj¾;kh fkdlrhs' th
    uq¿ jevigyku iuia;hla f,i mßj¾;kh lrhs' th iïmQ¾K uQ,dY% fla;h ilihs" fodaI mÍlaId
    lrhs" miqj l%shd;aul l< yels f.dkqjla fyda hka;% fla;hla ckkh lrhs'
    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    This statement is correct. Assembly language is a low level programming language. An assembler is a
    program that converts assembly language into machine code. The machine code is in binary form, which
    can be directly understood and executed by a computer’s CPU.
    fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈhs' tfiïí,s NdIdj my;a uÜgfï l%uf,aLk NdIdjls' tfiïí,rhla hkq tfiïí,s
    NdIdj" hka;% fla;hla njg mßj¾;kh lrk jevigykls' hka;% fla;h oaúuh wdldrfhka jk w;r
    th mß.Klhl CPU u.ska fl,skau f;areï f.k l%shd;aul l< yelsh'
    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    This statement is incorrect. An interpreter does not translate the entire program into machine code at once.
    Instead, it translates the program line by line during execution. It reads a line, converts it to machine code,
    executes it, and then moves on to the next line. This makes interpreters slower than compilers for the same
    program, but they offer advantages in terms of flexibility and ease of debugging.
    fuu m%ldYh jerÈhs' w¾: úkHdilhla uq¿ jevigyku tljr hka;% fla;hg mßj¾;kh fkdlrhs'
    tA fjkqjg" th l%shd;aul lsÍfï§ l%uf,aL fma<sfhka fma<shg mßj¾;kh lrhs' th fma<shla lshjd"
    hka;% fla;hg mßj¾;kh lr" th l%shd;aul lr" B<Õ fma<shg hhs' fuh w¾: úkHdilh" iïmdolhg
    jvd ukao.dó lrhs' kuq;a tAjd kuHYS,SNdjh iy ksfodialrKfha myiqj wkqj jdis ,nd fohs'

 6. What is the output of the following Python code segment if executed with 30 as input?

          result = 5

    Explanation

    The user inputs 30, so n = 30.
    mßYS,lhd 30 wdodkh lrhs" tneúka n = 30 fõ'

    The first condition checks if n < 40.
    m<uq fldkafoaish n < 40 oehs mÍlaId lrhs'

    Since 30 < 40 is True, execution enters the first if block.
    30 < 40 i;H neúka" l%shd;aul lsÍu m<uq if íf,dla tlg we;=¿ fõ'

    Inside this block, result is initially set to 1.
    fuu íf,dla tl ;=<" m%;sM,h uq,ska 1 f,i ilid we;'

    The nested if condition checks if (n < 10).

 8. What is the output of the following Python code segment?

                continue
          s = s + aList[i]
    print s

                                                                                        ( 2018 - 48 )
    Answer: 4)

    Code Analysis / fla;h úYaf,aIKh

    aList = [2, 3, 11, 13, 5, 7] # List of numbers
    s = 0 # Initialize sum to 0
    for i in range(len(aList)):
        if aList[i] > 10: # Skip numbers greater than 10
            continue
        s = s + aList[i] # Add the remaining numbers to s
    print(s) # Print the final sum

    Step-by-step Execution / mshjfrka mshjr l%shd;aul lsÍu

    List: [2, 3, 11, 13, 5, 7]

    Iteration & Condition Checking / mqkrdj¾;kh iy ;;a;ajh mÍlaId lsÍu

    2 → Added to s → s = 2
    3 → Added to s → s = 5
    11 → Skipped (greater than 10)
    13 → Skipped (greater than 10)
    5 → Added to s → s = 10
    7 → Added to s → s = 17

    Final Output / wjidk m%;sodkh : 17

    by; fla;fhys l%uf,aLl úiska w¾:olajk ,o ‘sum’ kue;s Y%s;h wvx.= úh hq;= h' ‘sum’ kue;s
    Y%s;h ksjerÈj w¾: oelaùu i|yd 2 jk fma<sfhys ysia;ekg my; oelafjk l=ula we;=<;a l< hq;=o?

    Answer: 2)

    This question is about defining functions.
    fuu m%Yakh Y%s; ye|skaùu ms<sn| fõ'

    We can see that the function adds two variables and stores it in another variable.
    Y%s;h úp,H folla tl;= lr fjk;a úp,Hhl .nvd lrk nj wmg oel.; yel'

    Also in line 6, we can see the function is called and two inputs are given to it.
    ;jo 6 jk fma<sfha" Y%s;h le|jd wdodk folla thg ,nd oS we;s njo wmg oelsh yel'

    If the function can get two inputs, then there must be two defined variables in the function definition.
    Y%s;hg wdodk folla ,nd.; yels kï" ye|skajQ úp,H folla Y%s;fha ye|skaùu ;=< mej;sh hq;=h'

    Since the variable 's' is defined in line 3, arg1 and arg2 are the only variables never defined. Also to get
    the sum of 10 and 20, they must be the arg1 and arg2 at that moment.
    3 jk fma<sfhaoS 's' úp,Hh y÷kajd we;s neúka" y÷kajd ke;af;a arg1 iy arg2 úp,Hhka muKs' ;jo
    10 ys iy 20 ys tl;=j ,nd .ekSug" tajd tu fudfydf;aoS arg1 yd arg2 úh hq;=h'

    Considering all of these reasons, the correct code to define function 'sum' is;
    fï ish¨u fya;= ie,lSfuka" 'sum' Y%s;h ye|skaùug ksjeros fla;h jkafka;
    def sum(arg1, arg2):

    Why other options are incorrect / wfkl=;a úl,am jerÈ jkafka wehs

    1)sum(arg1, arg2):
    This is incorrect because the def keyword is missing.
    def uQ, moh fkdue;s ksid fuh jerÈhs'

    3)function sum(arg1, arg2):
    Python does not use function to define functions (this syntax is similar to JavaScript).
    mhs;ka" Y%s; ks¾jpkh lsÍug Y%s;h Ndú;d fkdlrhs ^fuu jdlH LKavh JavaScript j,g iudk fõ&'

    4)def sum(arg1, arg2, s):
    The function only needs two parameters (arg1 and arg2). The variable s is defined inside the function.
    Y%s;hg wjYH jkafka mrdñ;s folla muKs (arg1 iy arg2).úp,Hh Y%s;h ;=< w¾: olajd we;'

    5)def sum( ):
    This defines a function with no parameters, but the function requires two arguments.
    fuh mrdñ;Ska fkdue;s Y%s;hla w¾: olajhs" kuq;a Y%s;hg ;¾l folla wjYH fõ'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 2.
    tneúka" ksjeros ms<s;=r 2 fõ'

 5. What is the value of the following Python expression?
    my; oelafjk mhs;ka m%ldYfhys w.h l=ula o?

    (100 // 3) % 4 | 8

    1) 0                 2) 0.125          3) 3                4) 8               5) 9

                                                                                                 ( 2019 - 44 )
    Answer : 5)

    This calculation will be done in according to the operator precedence in Python
    fuu .Kkh lsÍu mhs;ka ys l%shd m%uqL;djhg wkqj isÿ flf¾'

    Operator precedence determines the order of operations when an expression contains multiple operators.
    In Python, operations with higher precedence are executed before operations with lower precedence.
    m%ldYkhl .Ks; fufyhqï lsysmhla wvx.= jk úg l%shd m%uqL;djh fufyhqï wkqms<sfj, ;SrKh
    lrhs' mhs;ka ys" wvq m%uqL;djhla iys; fufyhqï j,g fmr jeä m%uqL;djhla iys; fufyhqï l%shd;aul
    fõ'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -85-                                           #ictfromabc

         Precedence
                             Operators                          Description                     Associativity
            Rank
         1 (Highest)                ()                   Parentheses (Grouping)                  Left to Right
              2                     **                   Exponentiation (Power)                  Right to Left
                                                 Multiplication, Division, Floor Division,
             3                *, /, //, %                                                        Left to Right
                                                                 Modulus
             4                   +, -                     Addition, Subtraction                  Left to Right
             5                  <<, >>                   Bitwise Shift Left, Right               Left to Right
             6                    &                           Bitwise AND                        Left to Right
             7                     ^                          Bitwise XOR                        Left to Right
             8                     |                           Bitwise OR                        Left to Right
                           ==, !=, >, <, >=,
             9                                             Comparison Operators                  Left to Right

              10                 not                           Logical NOT                       Right to Left
              11                 and                           Logical AND                       Left to Right
         12 (Lowest)              or                            Logical OR                       Left to Right

    Step 1
    Solve (100//3)
    The // operator in Python means integer division. It focuses on the integer part of the answer, ignoring the
    remainder part.
    Python ys // hkafkka woyia lrkafka ksÅ, fn§uhs' th ms<s;=f¾ ksÅ, fldgi flfrys wjOdkh
    fhduq lrk w;r b;sß fldgi fkdi,ld yßhs'
    100//3 = 33

    Step 2
    Solve (100//3) % 4
    The % operator in Python means Modulus division.
    Python ys % fufyhqfuka woyia jkafka b;sß fn§u hkakhs'
    It only focuses on the remainder part of the answer.
    th ms<s;=f¾ b;sß fldgi flfrys muKla wjOdkh fhduq lrhs'
    (100//3) % 4 = 33 % 4
                     =1       (33/4 is 8 with a remainder of 1)

    Step 3
    Solve 1 | 8
    The | operator means Bitwise OR.
    ‘|’ hkafkka woyia lrkafka ìgq wkqidß; OR hkakhs'
    The bitwise OR operation compares corresponding bits of two numbers and returns 1 if at least one of
    the bits is 1, otherwise it returns 0.
    ìgq wkqidß; OR fufyhqu uÕska ixLHd foll wkqrEm ìgq ixikaokh lr wju jYfhka ìgq tlla
    fyda 1 kï 1 ,nd fohs" tfia fkdue;skï 0 ,nd fohs'

    Solving for 1 | 8
    1 = 0001 (in 4-bit binary)
    8 = 1000 (in 4-bit binary)

     0        0        0        1
     1        0        0        0
     1        0        0        1

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -86-                                           #ictfromabc

    Each bit is compared
    iEu ìgqjlau ixikaokh lr we;
    0 OR 1 = 1
    0 OR 0 = 0
    0 OR 0 = 0
    1 OR 0 = 1
    Therefore: 1 | 8 = 1001

    1001 is 9 in decimal
    1001 hkq oYuh ixLHd j,ska 9 fõ

    Output /m%;sodkh 9

    Thus, the correct answer is 5)
    fï wkqj" ksjerÈ ms,s;=r 5)

 8. What will be the output of the following Python code segment?

    L = [1,-2,4,3,2,-7,11,2,8,-1]

                      2020 AL PYTHON MCQ MARKING

   1. Which of the following statements regarding compilers and interpreters are correct?
      iïmdol (compiler) iy mßj¾;l (interpreter) ms<sn|j my; ljr m%ldY i;H fõ o?
          A. A compiler transforms an entire high-level language program into its machine code.
             iïmdolhla" by< uÜgfï (high level) NdIdjlska ,shejqkq uq¿ l%uf,aLhu hka;% fla;hg yrjhs'
          B. An interpreter converts each high-level program statement into the relevant machine code during
             the program run. / mßj¾;lhla" l%uf,aLhla l%shd;aul lsÍfï § (execute/run) by< uÜgfï
             l%uf,aLfhys tla tla j.ka;sh (statement) ne.ska f.k" th wod< hka;% fla;hg yrjhs'
          C. Compiled codes usually run faster than interpreted codes.
             idudkHfhka iïmdokh jQ fla;" mßj¾;kh jQ fla;j,g jvd jeä fõ.hlska Odjkh fõ'

          1) A only.                     2) A and B only.                3) A and C only.               4) B and C only.                5) All of the above.
             A muKs.                        A yd B muKs.                    A yd C muKs.                   B yd C muKs.                    by; ish,a,u.

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2020 - 16 )
          Answer : 5 )
          Compiler / iïmdol
          A compiler is a program that translates code written in a high-level programming language (like C, Java,
          or Python) into machine code (binary code) that a computer’s processor can understand and execute. This
          translation enables the program to run on the hardware without requiring further interpretation.
          iïmdolhla hkq by< uÜgfï l%uf,aLk NdIdjlska ^C, Java fyda Python jeks& ,shd we;s fla;h
          mß.Klfha ilikhg f;areï f.k l%shd;aul l< yels hka;% fla;h ^oaúuh fla;h& njg mßj¾;kh
          lrk jevigykls' fuu mßj¾:kh ;jÿrg;a w¾:l:kh lsÍulska f;drj oDVdx.h u; l%shd lsÍug
          jevigykg yelshdj ,nd fohs'

          Key Functions of a Compiler /iïmdolhl m%Odk ld¾hhka

          Translation of Entire Code / iïmQ¾K fla;h mßj¾;kh
          A compiler takes the entire source code of a program as input and translates it into machine code all at
          once, creating an executable file.
          iïmdolhla jevigykl iïmQ¾K uQ, fla;h wdodkh f,i f.k th tljr hka;% fla;hg mßj¾;kh
          lr l%shd;aul l< yels f.dkqjla ks¾udKh lrhs'
          This executable file can be run independently without needing the compiler to be present, which makes
          compiled programs run faster.
          fuu l%shd;aul l< yels f.dkqj iïmdolh wjYH fkdù iajdëkj Odjkh l< yels w;r tuÕska
          iïmdokh lrk ,o jevigyka fõ.fhka l%shd;aul fõ'

          Syntax and Semantic Analysis /iskagelaia iy w¾: úYaf,aIK
          The compiler performs syntax analysis to check for any grammatical errors in the code and semantic
          analysis to ensure the code’s logic is consistent with the language rules.
          iïmdokh lrkakd úiska fla;fha lsishï jHdlrK fodaIhla ;sfíoehs mÍlaId lsÍug jdlH úYaf,aIK isÿ
          lrk w;r fla;fha ;¾lkh NdId Í;sj,g wkql+, nj iy;sl lsÍu i|yd w¾: úYaf,aIK isÿ lrhs'
          If errors are found, they are reported, and the program won’t compile until the errors are corrected.
          fodaI yuq jqjfyd;a" tAjd jd¾;d lrkq ,nk w;r" fodaI ksjerÈ lrk ;=re jevigyk iïmdokh fkdflf¾'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -94-                                          #ictfromabc

    Optimization / m%Yia;lrKh
    Compilers often optimize the code to make it run more efficiently on the target hardware.
    iïmdolhska fndfyda úg b,lal.; oDvdx. u; jvd;a ld¾hlaIuj Odjkh lsÍug fla;h m%Yia; lrhs'
    This can involve reducing memory usage, improving execution speed, and removing redundant code.
    u;l Ndú;h wvq lsÍu" l%shd;aul lsÍfï fõ.h jeä ÈhqKq lsÍu iy w;sßla; fla;h bj;a lsÍu
    fuhg we;=<;a úh yelsh'

    Linking and Code Generation / iïnkaO lsÍu iy fla; W;amdokh
    The compiler generates the machine code (also called object code) and links any necessary library files,
    creating a fully executable file.
    iïmdolh hka;% fla;h ckkh lrhs ^jia;= fla;h f,io ye¢kafõ& iy wjYH ´kEu mqia;ld, f.dkq
    iïnkaO lr" iïmq¾Kfhkau l%shd;aul l< yels f.dkqjla ks¾udKh lrhs'

    Advantages of Using a Compiler / iïmdolhla Ndú;d lsÍfï jdis

    •   Faster Execution /fõ.j;a l%shd;aul lsÍu
        Since the code is pre-compiled into machine code, it runs faster compared to interpreted code.
        fla;h hka;% fla;hg fmr iïmdokh lr we;s neúka" th w¾:l:kh lrk ,o fla;hg idfmalaIj
        fõ.fhka l%shd lrhs'
    •   Error Detection / fodaI y÷kd.ekSu
        Compilers catch syntax and some logical errors before execution, which makes programs more robust
        and easier to debug.
        iïmdokh lrkakka l%shd;aul lsÍug fmr jdlH LKav iy iuyr ;d¾lsl fodaI w,a,d .kS" tuÕska
        jevigyka jvd;a Yla;su;a iy ksfodia lsÍu myiq lrhs'
    •   Standalone Executable / iajdëk l%shd;aul l< yels
        Compilers create standalone executables that can be distributed and run on compatible systems
        without needing the source code or compiler.
        iïmdolhska úiska m%Nj fla;h fyda iïmdolh wjYH fkdù fnod yeßh yels iy wkql+, moaO;s
        u; Odjkh l< yels iajdëk l%shd;aul l< yels ks¾udKh lrhs'

    Example of Compiled Languages / iïmdokh lrk ,o NdId i|yd WodyrKh
    Languages like C, C++, and Java (although Java also involves a Just-In-Time compiler) are commonly
    compiled. This means code is turned into an executable file, which can be directly run on target machine.
    C, C++ iy Java jeks NdId ^Java ys Just-In-Time compiler tlla o we;=<;a jqjo& idudkHfhka
    iïmdokh flf¾' fuhska woyia lrkafka fla;h l%shd;aul l< yels f.dkqjla njg m;a lr we;s w;r
    th b,lal hka;%h u; fl,skau l%shd;aul l< yels njhs'

    Interpreter / mßj¾;l
    An interpreter is a program that reads and executes code written in a high-level programming language
    (like Python, Ruby, or JavaScript) line by line or statement by statement, without compiling the entire
    code into machine code beforehand.
    mßj¾;lhla hkq iïmQ¾K fla;h hka;% fla;hg fmr iïmdokh fkdlr by< uÜgfï l%uf,aLk
    NdIdjlska ^Python, Ruby fyda JavaScript jeks& fma<sfhka fyda m%ldYfhka m%ldYfhka ,shd we;s fla;h
    lshjd l%shd;aul lrk jevigykls'

    Key Functions of an Interpreter / mßj¾;lfhl=f.a m%Odk ld¾hhka

    •   Line-by-Line Execution / fma<sfhka fma<sh l%shd;aul lsÍu
        Unlike a compiler, an interpreter reads and translates each line of code into machine code individually
        and executes it immediately.
        iïmdolfhl= fuka fkdj" mßj¾;lfhl= úiska tla tla fla; fma<sh ;ks ;ksj hka;% fla;hg
        lshjd mßj¾;kh lr th jydu l%shd;aul lrhs'
        The program is executed in real-time, meaning that if an error occurs on one line, the interpreter will
        stop and report the error without attempting to execute the remaining code.
        jevigyk ;;H ld,Skj l%shd;aul fõ" thska woyia jkafka tla fma<shl fodaIhla isÿjqjfyd;a"
        mßj¾;lhd b;sß fla;h l%shd;aul lsÍug W;aidy fkdlr fodaIh jd¾;d lrk njhs'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -95-                                           #ictfromabc

    •   No Standalone Executable / iajdëkj l%shd;aul l< fkdyel
        Since an interpreter does not compile the entire program, there is no standalone executable file
        produced. Each time the program runs, it must be interpreted, which often makes interpreted programs
        slower compared to compiled ones.
        mßj¾;lfhl= úiska iïmQ¾K jevigyk iïmdokh fkdlrk neúka" iajdëkj l%shd;aul l< yels
        f.dkqjla ksIamdokh fkdfõ' jevigyk l%shd;aul jk iEu wjia:djlu" th w¾:l:kh l< hq;=
        w;r" fndfyda úg iïmdokh lrk ,o tAjdg idfmalaIj w¾:l:kh lrk ,o jevigyka ukao.dófõ'

    •   Error Reporting During Execution / l%shd;aul lsÍfï§ fodaI jd¾;d lsÍu
        Errors are detected and reported as each line of code is executed. This can make debugging easier for
        some types of programs, as developer can see errors immediately without needing to recompile code.
        iEu fla; fma<shlau l%shd;aul jk úg fodaI y÷kdf.k jd¾;d flfra' ixj¾Olhdg fla;h kej;

    Advantages of Using an Interpreter / mßj¾;lhla Ndú;d lsÍfï jdis

    •   Immediate Execution and Testing / laIKslj l%shd;aul lsÍu iy mÍlaId lsÍu

        debugging, as changes can be tested instantly without a full compile cycle.

        ksfodialrKfha§ úfYaIfhka WmldÍ fõ" iïmQ¾K iïmdok pl%hla fkdue;sj fjkialï laIKslj
        mÍlaId l< yelsh'
    •   Portability / wf;a f.k hd yels yelshdj
        Since interpreted code is run by an interpreter rather than compiled directly into machine-specific
        code, it can often be run on any system with the appropriate interpreter installed.
        mßj¾:kh lrk ,o fla;h hka;%-úfYaIs; fla;hlg fl,skau iïmdokh lrkjdg jvd
        mßj¾;lfhl= úiska l%shd;aul lrk neúka" th fndfyda úg iqÿiq mßj¾;lh ia:dmkh lr we;s
        ´kEu moaO;shl Odjkh l< yelsh'

    Disadvantages of Interpreters / mßj¾;lhkaf.a wjdis

    •   Lack of Standalone Executable / iajdëkj l%shd;aul lsÍfï yelshdj fkdue;slu

 3. For the algorithm to function correctly, what should be inserted at the blank (Q)?
    we,af.dß;uh ksjerÈj l%shd;aul ùu i|yd kï ysia;ekg l=ula we;=<;a l< hq;= o?

    1) Is i <n?           2) Is i = n?           3) Is count < n?       4) Is count < K?       5) Is n > 0?

                                                                                                      ( 2020 - 33 )
    Answer : 1 )

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    The Q blank in this question is used for the condition check in the loop. This condition ensures that the
    algorithm continues checking elements of L until all have been evaluated. A correct condition here is
    crucial to prevent out-of-bounds errors.
    fuu m%Yakfha we;s Q ysia;ek Æmfha ;;a;ajh mÍlaId lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d fõ' we,af.dß;u ish,a,
    we.hSug ,la lrk f;la L ys uQ,øjH mÍlaId lsÍu È.gu lrf.k hk nj fuu fldkafoaish iy;sl
    lrhs' iSudfjka msg; fodaI je<elaùu i|yd fuys ksjerÈ fldkafoaish b;d jeo.;a fõ'

    So, Option (1) Is i < n ? , is the correct choice because it ensures that the loop stops when i reaches n ,
    which is the length of the list L. This guarantees that the loop only iterates over valid indices, thus enabling
    all elements in L to be checked against K.
    tuksid" úl,amh (1) Is i < n o ?" ksjerÈ f;aÍu jkafka th i n fj; <Õd jk úg Æmh k;r jk
    nj iy;sl lrk ksidh" th L ,ehsia;=fõ È. fõ' fuh j,x.= o¾Yl u; muKla mqkrdj¾;kh jk
    nj iy;sl lrhs" tuÕska L ys we;s ish¨u uQ,øjH K g tfrysj mÍlaId lsÍug yels fõ'

    Therefore the correct answer is 1).
    tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 1) fõ'

    Code Example for Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu i|yd fla;uh WodyrKh'

        i = 0
        while i < n:
            if L[i] == K:
                count = count + 1
            i = i + 1

 5. What would be the output of the following Python code, if the input was 17?
    my; oelafjk mhs;ka fla;fhys wdodkh 17 jQ úg m%;sodkh l=ula o?
    n = float(input())
    m = (n // (n % 5)) ** 3
    print(m)

    1) 24.0             2) 25.5             3) 512.0               4) 614.125            5) an error message

                                                                                                    ( 2020 - 35 )
    Answer : 3 )

    Modulus Calculation / udmdxl .Kkh
    The finding the remainder when one number is divided by another number is known as modulus
    calculation. It is useful in many applications, such as checking if a number is even or odd, patterns,
    or handling periodic tasks in code.
    tla wxlhla ;j;a wxlhlska fn¥ úg b;sßh fidhd .ekSu udmdxl .Kkh f,i ye¢kafõ' wxlhla
    brÜfÜ fyda T;af;a o hkak mÍlaId lsÍu" rgd fyda fla;fha wdj¾;s;d ld¾hhka yeisrùu jeks fndfyda
    fhÿïj, th m%fhdackj;a fõ'

    Floor Division / ksÅ, fnosu
    Dividing two numbers and rounding down the result to the nearest whole number. In programming,
    the floor division operator is typically represented as //.
    ixLHd folla fn§u iy m%;sM,h wdikak;u mQ¾K ixLHdjg jg heúu ksÅ, fnosu f,i yeoskafú'
    l%uf,aLkfha§" ksÅ, fn§fï fufyhjkh idudkHfhka // f,i ksrEmKh flf¾'

    Exponentiation / n,h ,nd osu
    Raising a number to the power of another number is known as exponentiation. It is represented
    mathematically as ab, where a is the base and b is the exponent.
    wxlhla fjk;a wxlhl n,hg kexùu n,h ,nd osu f,i yeoskafõ' th ab f,i .Ks;uh jYfhka
    ksrEmKh jk w;r" a hkq mdoh jk w;r b hkq n,h ,nd osu fõ'

    In programming, exponentiation is typically denoted by the ** operator.
    l%uf,aLkfha§" n,h ,nd osu idudkHfhka ** fufyhjkh úiska olajkq ,efí'

    Let's analyze this Python code / fuu mhs;ka fla;h úYaf,aIKh lruq'

    •   n = float(input())
        This line takes an input, converts it to a floating-point number, and assigns it to n. If the input is 17,
        n will be 17.0.
        fuu fma<sh wdodkhla f.k th bmsf,k ,laIH ixLHdjla njg mßj¾;kh lr th n fj;
        mjrhs' wdodkh 17 kï" n, 17.0 fõ'

    •   m = (n // (n % 5)) ** 3:

        a) Modulus Calculation / udmdxl .Kkh (n % 5)
           Calculates 17.0 % 5, which gives 2.0 (the remainder when 17 is divided by 5).
           17.0 % 5 .Kkh lrhs" th 2.0 ,nd fohs ^17, 5 ka fn¥ úg ,efnk b;sßh&'
        b) Floor Division / ksÅ, fnosu (n // (n % 5))
           Calculates 17.0 // 2.0, resulting in 8.0 (since // performs floor division).
           17.0 // 2.0 .Kkh lrhs" tys m%;sM,hla f,i 8.0 ^$$ ìu fn§u isÿ lrk neúka&'
        c) Exponentiation / n,h ,nd osu (** 3)
           Raises 8.0 to the power of 3, giving 512.0.
           512.0 ,nd foñka 8.0, 3 ys n,fhka by< kxjhs'

    •   print(m)
        The final output for m is 512.0.
        m i|yd wjidk m%;sodkh 512.0 fõ'

 6. Suppose that S is a string, L is a list and T is a tuple in a Python program. The length of each is 10.
    Consider the following Python statements:
    hï mhs;ka l%uf,aLhla hkq ;ka;=jla (string) o" L hkq ,ehsia;=jla (list) o" T hkq Wm,elshdkhla (tuple)
    o fõ' ta tl tflys È. (length) 10 ls. my; mhs;ka m%ldY i,lkak'
    A - S[2] = '2'
    B - L[2] = '2'
    C - T[2] = '2'
    Which of the above three statements will generate an error?
    by; m%ldY ;=fkka ljrla fodaIhla ckkh (generate) lrkq ,nhs o?

     1) A only.         2) B only.          3) A and B only.    4) A and C only.   5) All of the above.
        A muKs.            B muKs.             A yd B muKs.        A yd C muKs.       by; ish,a,u.

                                                                                              ( 2020 - 36 )
    Answer : 4 )

    Let's evaluate each statement to determine if it would generate an error.
    tla tla m%ldYh fodaIhla ckkh lrhso hkak ;SrKh lsÍug we.hSug ,la lruq'

    Statement / m%ldYh I (S[2] = '2'):
    S is a string, and strings in Python are immutable. This means that their contents cannot be changed
    after they are created. Trying to assign a new value to an element in a string (like S[2] = '2') will
    raise a TypeError.
    S hkq string tlla jk w;r mhs;kays we;s string tl fjkia l< fkdyel' fuhska woyia lrkafka
    tAjd ks¾udKh lsÍfuka miq tAjdfha wka;¾.;h fjkia l< fkdyels njhs' string tll we;s uQ,dx.hlg
    kj w.hla mejÍug W;aidy lsÍu (S[2] = '2' jeks) TypeError tlla u;= lrhs'

    Result / m%;sM,h
    This statement will generate an error.
    fuu m%ldYh fodaIhla ckkh fkdlrkq we;'

        The values of p and q are swapped (p, q = q, p).

 10. What would be the output of the following Python code?
     my; oelafjk mhs;ka fla;fha m%;sodkh l=ula o?
    x = 1
    y = 100
    while (x < 100):
          y = y - x
          x = x + 1
          if (x + y) < 90:
                break
    print(y)

    1) 100               2) 85                3) 79                  4) 72              5) 7

                                                                                               ( 2020 - 40 )
    Answer : 3 )

    The program starts with x = 1 and y = 100. In each iteration of the loop, y is updated as y = y - x,
    and x is incremented by 1 (x = x + 1). The condition (x + y) < 90 is checked, and if true, the
    loop exits using the break statement.
    jevigyk wdrïN jkafka x = 1 iy y = 100' Æmfha iEu mqkrdj¾;khl§u" y = y – x, x f,i
    hdj;ald,Sk jk w;r (x = x + 1) 1lska jeä fõ' fldkafoaish (x + y) < 90 mÍlaId lr we;s
    w;r" i;H kï" break m%ldYh Ndú;fhka Æmh msgfjhs'

                                     Value of y                           Condition
        Iteration     Value of x                    Value of x + y                         Action
                                     y ys w.h                            fldkafoaish
      mqkrdj¾;kh      x ys w.h                      x + y ys w.h         (x + y) < 90
                                                                                           l%shdj
                                     (y = y - x)
             1             1        100 - 1 = 99   1 + 99 = 100        False            Continue loop
             2             2        99 - 2 = 97    2 + 97 = 99         False            Continue loop
             3             3        97 - 3 = 94    3 + 94 = 97         False            Continue loop
             4             4        94 - 4 = 90    4 + 90 = 94         False            Continue loop
             5             5        90 - 5 = 85    5 + 85 = 90         False            Continue loop
             6             6        85 - 6 = 79    6 + 79 = 85         True             Break the loop

    At the 6th iteration, y becomes 79 and x becomes 6, resulting in x + y = 85, which satisfies the
    condition. Thus, the loop terminates, and the final value of y is 79.
    6 jk mqkrdj¾;kfha §" y, 79njg m;a jk w;r x, 6 njg m;a fõ" tys m%;sM,hla f,i x + y
    = 85 fldkafoaish ;Dma;su;a fõ' fï wkqj" Æmh wjika jk w;r" y ys wjidk w.h 79 fõ'

    So, the correct Answer is 3).
    ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 3) fõ.

       code

    4) A - source code, B - object code, C - linker, D - compiler, E - executable code

    Answer : 5 )

    A Simple Explanation of Compiling, Linking, and Executing Code
    fla;h iïmdokh lsÍu" iïnkaO lsÍu iy l%shd;aul lsÍu ms<sn| ir, meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    1   Source Code / uQ,dY% fla;h
        This is the human-readable code you write, often in languages like C, C++ or Python. It contains
        instructions for the computer to follow. fuh fndfyda úg C, C++ fyda Python jeks NdIdj,ska
        ñksiqkag lshúh yels fla;hhs' mß.Klh wkq.ukh l< hq;= Wmfoia tys wvx.= fõ'
    2   Compiler / iïmdolh
        The compiler takes your source code and translates it into a language the computer can
        understand, which is usually machine code or assembly language. This translated code is called
        object code. iïmdolhd Tfí uQ,dY% fla;h f.k th mß.Klhg f;areï .; yels NdIdjlg
        mßj¾;kh lrhs" th idudkHfhka hka;% fla;h fyda tl,ia lsÍfï NdIdj fõ' fuu mßj¾;kh
        lrk ,o fla;h jia;= fla;h f,i ye¢kafõ'
    3   Object Code / jia;= fla;h
        Object code is the machine-readable version of your program. It contains instructions that the
        computer can execute, but it's often not complete. jia;= fla;h hkq Tfí jevigyfka hka;%fhka
        lshúh yels wkqjdohhs' mß.Klhg l%shd;aul l< yels Wmfoia tys wvx.= fõ" kuq;a th fndfyda
        úg iïmQ¾K fkdfõ'

 5. What would be the output after executing the following Python code?

                                                                                           ( 2021 - 41 )
    Answer : 2 )
    Here, n is assigned the value 117.
    fuys§ n g 117 w.h mjrkq ,efí'

    m = (n & 127) // (2 ** 3)

    •   Step 1:
        Bitwise AND Operation

        The expression n & 127 performs a bitwise AND operation between n and 127.
        n & 127 m%ldYkh n iy 127 w;r ìgq wkqidrs AND fufyhqula isÿ lrhs'

        •    The binary representation of 117 is 01110101.
             117 ys oaúuh ksrEmKh 01110101 fõ'
        •    The binary representation of 127 is 01111111.
             127 ys oaúuh ksrEmKh 01111111 fõ'

        Now, let's perform the bitwise AND operation:
        oeka" wms ìgq wkqidrs AND fufyhqu isÿ lruq(

          01110101
        & 01111111
          --------------
          01110101

        The result of n & 127 is 117.
        n & 127 ys m%;sM,h 117 fõ'

    •   Step 2
        Division by Power of 2 / 2 n,fhka fn§u
        Next, we calculate 2 ** 3, which is 8.
        B<Õg" wms 2 ** 3 .Kkh lruq" th 8 fõ'
        m = 117 // 8

    •   Step 3
        Integer Division / ksÅ, fn§u
        117 // 8 = 14

        This means 117 divided by 8 is 14.625, here we discard the remainder in integer division.
        fuhska woyia lrkafka 117, 8 ka fn§u 14.625 fõ" fuys§ wms ksÅ, fn§fï§ b;sßh bj;,uq'

    Hence the answer is 14.
    tneúka ms<s;=r 14 fõ'

 8. What will be the output when the following Python code is executed?

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                 -118-                                          #ictfromabc

     Flow charts often use three basic structures: sequence, selection, and repetition.
     .e,Sï igyka fndfyda úg uQ,sl jHqyhka ;=kla Ndú;d lrhs( wkql%uh" f;aÍu iy mqk¾lrKh'

     Sequence wkql%uh
     A sequence is a series of steps or actions that are performed in a specific order. Each step follows the
     previous one without any branching.
     wkql%uh hkq ksYaÑ; wkqms<sfj,lg isÿ lrkq ,nk mshjr fyda l%shd ud,djls' iEu mshjrlau lsisÿ
     YdLdjla fkdue;sj fmr mshjr wkq.ukh lrhs'

     Selection f;aÍu
     Selection involves making a choice between different paths based on a condition or decision. It is also
     known as a conditional or decision structure.
     f;arsu hkq fldkafoaishla fyda ;SrKhla u; mokïj úúO ud¾. w;r f;aÍula lsÍuhs' th
     fldkafoais iys; fyda ;SrK jHqyhla f,io ye¢kafõ'

     Repetition mqk¾lrKh
     Repetition involves repeating a set of steps multiple times until a condition is met. It is also known as a
     loop structure.
     mqk¾lrKh hkq fldkafoaishla imqrd,k f;la mshjr ud,djla lsysm j;djla mqkrdj¾;kh lsÍuhs'
     th Æma jHqyhla f,io ye¢kafõ'
     If there is a point where an arrow that is moving down comes from a different path and collides with an
     arrowhead, the repetition takes place from that point down.
     my,g .uka lrñka ;sfnk B;,hlg fjk;a ud¾.hlska meñK B ysila .eàula isÿ jk ia:dkhla
     mj;Skï tu ia:dkfha isg my,g mqk¾lrKh isÿ fõ'
     Let's study the above flow chart step by step as below.
     by; .e,Sï igyk mshjfrka mshjr my; mßÈ wOHkh lruq'

     a=10
     r=1
     Substituting 10 for a variable called a and 1 for r.
     a kï úp,Hhla i|yd 10 wdfoaY lsÍu iy r i|yd 1 wdfoaY lrkq ,nhs'
     Then an arrowhead collides with the downward moving arrow, so the repetition is then activated.
     bkamiq B;, ysila my,g .ukalrk B;,h u; .efgk neúka bka miq mqk¾lrKh l%shd;aul fõ'

     Input n
     Here the value for n is taken as input.
     fuys§ n i|yd w.hla wdodkh f,i ,nd .kshs'

     Is n < 0 ?
     Here the selection is implemented. If the condition is true then r=r+n will be moved and a=a-1 will be
     moved. If the condition is false, it goes directly to a=a-1.
     fuys§ f;aÍu l%shd;aul fjhs' fldkafoaish i;H jkafka kï r=r+n fj; .uka lr a=a-1 fj; .uka
     lrkq ,nhs' fldkafoaish i;H fkdfõ kï Rcqju a=a-1 fj; .uka lrhs'
     The condition is true only if the number n is less than 0.
     fldkafoaish i;H jkafka n kï ixLHdj 0 g jvd l=vd kï muKs'

     Is a >= 0 ?
     It is on this condition that repetition takes place again and again. If the condition is true, it moves down
     and outputs r and terminates the process. If the condition is not true then iterate over and over again until
     the condition is true.
     mqk¾lrKh kej; kej;;a isÿ ùu isÿ jkafka fuu fldkafoaish u; fõ' fldkafoaish i;H jqjfyd;a
     my,g .uka lr r m%;sodkh lr l%shdj,sh wjika lrkq ,nhs' fldkafoaish i;H fkdjqjfyd;a kej;
     kej;;a fldkafoaish i;H jk f;la mqk¾lrKh isÿ fõ'
     The condition is true only if the number a is greater than or equal to 0.
     fldkafoaish i;H jkafka a kï ixLHdj 0 g jvd úYd, fyda iudk kï muKs'

     by; .Kkh lsÍu isÿ lsÍugkï fufyhjk m%uqL;d wkqms<sfj< ms<sn|j wjfndaOhla mej;Su

             Subtraction                -

     1 **3
     1

 6. What would be the output of the following Python code?
    my; oelafjk mhs;ka fla;fha m%;sodkh l=ula fõ o?
     val = 9
     for i in range(5):
          for j in range(2, 3, 1):
                val += 1
                if (val % 2) == 0:
                      continue
                      val += 2
                else:
                      val += 2
     print(val)

     1) 18                2) 24                 3) 29                4) 38                   5) 39

                                                                                                     ( 2022 - 41 )
     Answer : 1 )
     for loop
     Here, a condition depending on a data sequence is introduced to the control variable.
     fuys§ md,k úp,Hhg o;a; wkqms<sfj,la wkqj fldkafoaishla y÷kajd fokq ,efí'
     Included instructions are executed exactly the number of times equivalent to the number of elements in
     the conditional data sequence.
     wka;¾.; Wmfoia iuQyh l%shd;aul jkafka yßhgu fldkafoaisfhys we;s o;a; wkql%ufha wjhj
     .Kkh iudk jdr m%udKhls'
     The common structure of the for loop in Python
     Python ys for loop ys fmdÿ jHqyh
     for variable in (sequence):
            statements

     Methods to obtain the data sequence of the condition
     fldkafoaisfha o;a; wkql%uh ,nd .; yels l%u
     1.   Using a range() function
     2.   Using a string
     3.   Using a tuple
     4.   Using a list
     5.   Using a dictionary
     6.   Using a file

     range() function
     Used to generate a list of integers in a specific range instantly.
     ksIaÑ; mrdihlg wh;a jk ksÅ, ,ehsia;=jla ,nd.ekSu i|yd Ndú; lrhs'
     This can be used in 3 ways.
     fuh wdldr 3lska Ndú;d l< yel'

     range(stop)
     range(start, stop)
     range(start, stop, step)

     The stop value here is always exclusive.
     fuys stop w.h iEu úgu exclusive fõ'
     The start value here is always inclusive. The default of this is 0.
     fuys start w.h iEu úgu inclusive fõ' fuys iïu; w.h 0 fõ'

     The default of the step value is +1.
     step w.fhys iïu; w.h +1 fõ'

     next repeating cycle depending on whether a condition is fulfilled.

 7. Which of the following is/are correct regarding Python functions?
    mhs;ka Y%s; iïnkaOfhka my; i|yka l=ula ksjerÈ jkafka o?
     A - A Python function can return a data structure that contains multiple values.
         mhs;ka Y%s;hlg w.hka iuQyhla we;s o;a; jHQyhla (data structure) m%;Hd.ukh (return) l<
         yels h'
     B - A Python function can be used without passing any parameters to it.
         mhs;ka Y%s;hla" thg lsisÿ mrdñ;s heùulska f;drj Ndú; l< yels h'
     C - Parameters can be passed to a python function by value or by reference.
         mhs;ka Y%s;hlg mrdñ;Sk"a w.hla (value) fyda fhduqjla (reference) fyd f,i heúh yels h'

     1) B only.           2) C only.           3) A and C only.     4) B and C only.     5) All of the above.
        B muKs.              C muKs.              A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.         by; ish,a,u.

                                                                                                     ( 2022 - 42 )
     Answer : 5 )

     A Python function can return a data structure that contains multiple values.
     mhs;ka Y%s;hlg w.hka iuQyhla we;s o;a; jHQyhla (data structure) m%;Hd.ukh (return) l< yelsh'

     A Python function can return a data structure even if there is only one return value. A list, tuple or string
     data structure can be returned as such.
     mhs;ka Y%s;hla ;=< wdmiq ,nd §fï w.hka mj;skafka tlla muKla jqjo tu.ska o;a; jHqyhla wdmiq
     ,nd §ug yelsfõ' ,ehsia;=jla" tuple fyda string wdldrfha o;a; jHqyhla tfia wdmiq ,nd Èh yel'

     That is, applying return 1 return 2 return 3 in three lines will output only 1 and return(1,2,3)
     should be applied instead.
     tkï return 1 return 2 return 3 f,i fma<s ;=kl fh§fuka 1 muKla m%;sodkh jk nj;a tA
     fjkqjg return(1,2,3) fhÈh hq;= njhs'

     Then (1, 2, 3) is output.
     túg (1, 2, 3) f,i m%;sodkh fjhs'

     So, this statement is correct.
     tneúka" fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈ h'

     A Python function can be used without passing any parameters to it.
     mhs;ka Y%s;hla" thg lsisÿ mrdñ;s heùulska f;drj Ndú; l< yels h'

     User defined functions can be of 4 types.
     mßYS,lhd úiska w¾: oelaúh yels Y%s; 4 ls'

     1. With parameters with return
         defining
         def myfunc(x, y):
             return x*y

         calling
         myfunc(2, 3)
         6

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                             -132-                                         #ictfromabc

    2. Without parameters without return
        defining
        def logo():
        print("**************")
        print("**ictfromabc**")
        print("**************")

        calling
        logo()
        **************
        **ictfromabc**
        **************

    3. Without parameters with return
        defining
        def func():
                return "Hello World"

        calling
        func()
        'Hello World'

    4. With parameters without return
        defining
        def func(x, y):
                print(x*y)

        calling
        func(5,2)
        10

    Focusing on the above 4 types, this statement can also be recognized as correct.
    by; j¾. 4 ms<sn|j wjOdkh fhduq lsÍfï§ fuu m%ldYho ksjeros nj y÷kd .; yel'

    Parameters can be passed to a Python function by value or by reference.

    We all know that a value can be passed directly when calling a Python function.
    mhs;ka Y%s;hla le|ùfï§ w.hla Rcqju ,nd Èh yels nj wm fyd¢kau okafkuq'

   1. What would be the output of the following Python code, if a = 10, b = 4, and c = 7?
      a = 10, b = 4 iy c = 7 jk úg my; mhs;ka fla;fha m%;sodkh l=ula fõo?
          ans = a % b + c // (a-b)
          print(ans)

          1) 3                             2) 5                             3) 7                             4) 9                             5) 11

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2023 - 39 )
          Answer : 1 )
          It is important to have an understanding of the operator precedence that guides the calculation.
          .Kkh lsÍu isÿ lsÍug fufyhjk m%uqL;d wkqms<sfj, ms<sn|j wjfndaOhla mej;Su jeo.;a fõ'
          operator precedence
          fufyhjk m%uqL;d wkqms<sfj,
          1.          Parentheses                           ()
          2.          Exponentiation                        **
          3.          Multiplication                        *
                      Division                              /
                      Integer division                      //
                      Modulus division                      %
          4.          Addition                              +
                      Subtraction                           -

          ans = a % b + c // (a-b)
          This is where any calculation takes place and it is substituted into the variable called ans.
          lsishï .Kkh lsÍula isÿù th ans kï úp,Hh fj; wdfoaY ùu fuys§ isÿ fõ'
          The calculation can be done as follows by substituting 10, 4, and 7 for a, b, and c in the above calculation.
          by; .Kkh lsÍfï a, b, yd c i|yd ms,sfj,ska 10, 4, iy 7 wdfoaY lrñka my; mßÈ .Kkh lsÍu
          isÿ l, yel'
          a % b + c // (a-b)
          10 % 4 + 7 // (10-4)
          10 % 4 + 7 // (6)
          2 + 7 // 6 #note 1
          2 + 1 #note 2
          3
          Then 3, substitutes to ans.
          bkamiq 3, ans fj; wdfoaY fjhs'
          print(ans)
          Then 3 is output.
          bkamiq 3 m%;sodkh fjhs'
          #note 1
          Here is the modulus division. It means dividing a number by another number and getting the remainder.
          fuys§ isÿ ù we;af;a b;sß fn§u fõ' tkï lsishï ixLHdjla fjk;a ixLHdjlska fnod tu.ska ,efnk
          b;sß w.h ,nd .ekSuhs'
          10 % 4
          2

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                             -133-                                          #ictfromabc

    #note 2
    What has happened here is integer division. That is, when a number is divided by another number, it gives
    an integer.
    fuys§ isÿù we;af;a mQ¾K ixLHd fn§u fõ' tkï lsishï ixLHdjla fjk;a ixLHdjlska fn¥ úg mQ¾K
    ixLHdjla ,nd fok njhs'
    7 // 6
    1
    So, the answer is 1).
    tuksid ms<s;=r 1) fõ'

 3. What would be the output of the following Python code, when it gets executed?
    my; mhs;ka fla;h l%shd;aul l< úg m%;sodkh l=ula fõo?

    Answer : 1 )
    User defined functions
    mßYS,lhd úiska w¾:olajk ,o Y%s;

    A better understanding of functions is given in the above question, so let's study the code line by line.
    Y%s; ms<sn|j jvd;a fyd| wjfndaOhla by; m%Yakfha§ ,nd § we;s neúka fuys§ fla;fha fma<sfhka
    fma<sh wOHkh lruq'

    def modify_string(input_string):
    A function called modify_string has been created with a parameter input_string.
    input_string f,i mrdñ;shla iu. modify_string kñka Y%s;hla ks¾udKh lr we;'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -135-                                          #ictfromabc

    input_string += " World" #note1
    Here, when calling the function, the "word" string is added to the value given for input_string. Here this
    will only work if a string is given for input_string. Otherwise, an error is generated.
    fuys§ Y%s;h le|ùfïÈ input_string i|yd ,nd fok w.hg "word" string tl tl;= lsÍu isÿ lrhs'
    fuys§ input_string i|yd string tlla ,nd ÿka úg muKla fuh l%shd;aul fjhs' tfia fkdue;s úg
    fodaIhla ckkh lrkq ,nhs'

    text = "Hello"
    The string "Hello" is substituted for the variable text.
    "Hello" string tl text kï úp,Hh i|yd wdfoaY lsÍu isÿ lrkq ,nhs'

    modify_string(text) #note2
    Here the function is called and given the variable text as its parameter, it concatenates the string " World"
    to the existing string "Hello". But here nothing is returned and also what is changed in the function is the
    local string input_string. So here the global variable is not affected at all. When calling the function, the
    value of text is only substituted into the input_string, so no global variables are called here.
    fuys§ Y%s;h le|jk w;r tys mrdñ;sh f,i text hk úp,Hh ,nd fok neúka tys mj;sk "Hello"
    hk string tlg " World" hk string fldgi ixhqla; lrhs' kuq;a fuys lsisjla wdmiq ,nd fkdfok

    Lists

    Lists can contain different types of data and can be deleted, modified, or inserted.

    we;=<;a lsÍug yelshdj we;'

    negative indexes from -1.

    isg RK o¾Yl Ndú;fhkq;a m%fõY úh yels fõ'

    id. Here two variables are created.

 5. How many '⁕'s does this program output?
    my; l%uf,aLh" '⁕' fldmuK m%udKhla m%;sodkh lrhs o?
    i = 7
    while i>0:
          i -= 3
          print('⁕')
          if i<=2:
                 break
            else:
                 print('⁕')

    1) 1                  2) 3                  3) 5                   4) 7                  5) 9

                                                                                                     ( 2023 - 43 )
    Answer : 2 )
    In the above code, there is a while loop and an if statement inside that while loop.
    by; fla;h ;=, while Æmhla yd tu while Æmh ;=< if m%ldY mj;S'

    while loop
    Here the iteration takes place as long as any condition is true. There is a control variable that precedes the
    condition and increases or decreases within the while loop.
    fuys§ lsishï fldkafoaishla i;H ù ;sfnk ;dla mqkrdj¾;kh isÿ fõ' tys§ md,k úp,Hhla mj;sk
    w;r th fldkafoaishg fmr mj;sk w;r jeäùula fyda wvqùula o while Æmh ;=< mj;S'
    control variable
    while (condition):
            statements
            increment/decrement

    i = 7
    This is the control variable of the while loop as this is also specified in the condition in the while loop.
    while Æmh ;=< fldkafoaish ;=,o i|yka jkafka fuh jk neúka fuh while Æmfha md,k úp,Hh
    fjhs'

    while i>0:
    This means that as long as the value of i is greater than 0, the while loop will run. Also, since the value of
    i is already 7, the first iteration is executed.
    i ys w.h 0 g jvd úYd, ù ;sfnk ;dla while Æmh l%shd;aul jk nj fuu.ska meyeÈ,s fõ' tfiau
    oekgu;a i ys w.h 7 neúka m,uq mqkrdj¾;kh l%shd;aul fõ'

    i -= 3
    At each iteration of the while loop, i is subtracted by 3. This can also be expressed as follows.
    while Æmfha tla tla mqkrdj¾;k wjia:dfõ§ i f.ka 3 ne.ska wvq lrhs'fuh my; mßÈ oelaúh yel'
    i = i-3

    print('⁕')
    Outputs * at the appropriate iteration time.
    wod< mqkrdj¾;k wjia:dfõ§ * m%;sodkh lrhs'

    if i<=2:
             break
    If the value of i is 2 or less, the break command is executed.
    i ys w.h 2 fyda Bg jvd wvq fõ kï break úOdkh l%shd;aul fõ'

    The break command terminates the loop if the corresponding condition is true and moves to the code
    outside the loop. Since there are no lines of code after the for loop here, if this is true the code will
    terminate. In addition, there is a continue command. What happens is that if the condition is true, it moves
    to the next iteration regardless of the lines of code in the loop below the condition.

    break úOdkh u.ska isÿ jkafka wod< fldkafoaish i;H fõ kï Æmh wjika lr Æmfhka msg; fla;h

 6. Which of the data structures among Dictionary, List and Tuple in Python could be used to store a
    collection of key-value pairs where the keys must be unique?
    wkkH h;=re iys; h;=re-w.h (key-value) hq., tl;=jla .nvd lsÍug mhs;kays Dictionary, List
    iy Tuple o;a; jHqy w;=ßka l=ula Ndú; l< yels o?

    1) Dictionary only                       2) List only                               3) Tuple only
    4) Dictionary and List only              5) List and Tuple only
                                                                                                  ( 2023 - 44 )
    Answer : 1 )
    As mentioned in the question above, the key-valued data structure is a dictionary. The common structure
    of a dictionary
    by; m%Yakfha i|yka lr ;sfnk mßÈ h;=re-w.h f,i mj;sk o;a; jHqyh jkafka dictionary fõ'
    dictionary tll fmdÿ jHqyh
    {key1 : value1, key2 : value2}
    Here the keys must be unique and only integers or strings can be used for keys. Duplications are not
    supported here.
    fuys h;=re w.hka wksjdrfhkau wkkH úh hq;= w;r h;=re i|yd Ndú; l, yelafla ksÅ, fyda
    string muKs' fuys§ wkqmsgm;a ùï j,g iydh fkdolajhs'
    In a list, comma-separated data are enclosed in square brackets. Also, the elements in the list do not need
    to be unique and are not key-values here.
    ,ehsia;=jla ;=< fldud fhdod fjka l, o;a; fldgq jryka ;=, wka;¾.;j mj;S' tfiau tys§ ,ehsia;=j
    ;=< mj;sk uQ,dx. wkkH ùula wjYH fkdjk w;r fuys h;=re-w.hka f,i o fkdue;'
    In a tuple, comma-separated data are enclosed in round brackets. Also, the elements in the list do not need
    to be unique and are not key-values here.
    tuple tlla ;=< fldud fhdod fjka l, o;a; rjqï jryka ;=, wka;¾.;j mj;S' tfiau tys§ ,ehsia;=j
    ;=< mj;sk uQ,dx. wkkH ùula wjYH fkdjk w;r fuys h;=re-w.hka f,i o fkdue;'
    So, the answer is 1).
    tuksid ms<s;=r 1) fõ'

 7. What would be the output of the following python code?
    my; mhs;ka fla;fha m%;sodkh l=ula fõo?
    for i in range(1, 4):
          for j in range (1, i + 1):
                print(j * i, end=' ')
          print()

    1) 1                 2) 1                  3) 1                 4) 1 2 3                5) 1 2
       2 2                  2 4                   2 4                  2 4 6                   2 4 6
       3 3 3                3 6                   3 6 9                3 6 9                   3 6 9 12

                                                                                                 ( 2021 - 45 )
    Answer : 3 )
    for loop
    Here, a condition depending on a data sequence is introduced to the control variable.
    fuys§ md,k úp,Hhg o;a; wkqms<sfj,la wkqj fldkafoaishla y÷kajd fokq ,efí'
    Included instructions are executed exactly the number of times equivalent to the number of elements in
    the conditional data sequence.
    wka;¾.; Wmfoia iuQyh l%shd;aul jkafka yßhgu fldkafoaisfhys we;s o;a; wkql%ufha wjhj
    .Kkh iudk jdr m%udKhls'
    The common structure of the for loop in Python
    Python ys for loop ys fmdÿ jHqyh
    for variable in (sequence):
          statements

    Methods to obtain the data sequence of the condition
    fldkafoaisfha o;a; wkql%uh ,nd .; yels l%u
    1.   Using a range() function
    2.   Using a string
    3.   Using a tuple
    4.   Using a list
    5.   Using a dictionary
    6.   Using a file

    range() function
    Used to generate a list of integers in a specific range instantly.
    ksIaÑ; mrdihlg wh;a jk ksÅ, ,ehsia;=jla ,nd.ekSu i|yd Ndú; lrhs'

    This can be used in 3 ways.
    fuh wdldr 3lska Ndú;d l< yel'

    range(stop)
    range(start, stop)
    range(start, stop, step)

    The stop value here is always exclusive.
    fuys stop w.h iEu úgu exclusive fõ'

    The start value here is always inclusive. The default of this is 0.
    fuys start w.h iEu úgu inclusive fõ' fuys iïu; w.h 0 fõ'

    The default of the step value is +1.
    step w.fhys iïu; w.h +1 fõ'

    Now let's study the code in question line by line.
    oeka m%Yakfha we;s fla;h fma<sfhka fma<sh wOHkh lruq'

    for i in range(1, 4):
    1 to 4 (1, 2, 3) are substituted for i at each iteration.
    tla tla mqkrdj¾;k jdrfhaÈ i i|yd ms,sfj,ska 1 isg 4 olajd (1, 2, 3) wdfoaY fjhs'

    Æmh ;=< l%shd;aul fõ'

    l%shd;aul lsÍfïÈ ,efnk m%;sodkh;a tu fma<sh ;=<u o¾Ykh fõ' fuh fojk for Æmh hgf;a
    l%shd;aul fjhs'

    print()
    This is executed under the first for loop. This is used to prevent the rest of the output from occurring on
    the same line after the second for loop above has finished executing completely simultaneously. This will
    output an empty line. But here earlier the output was as end=" " so that empty line will be output on the
    same line. But since there is no end=" " here, the next output moves to the next line.

    tljrla l%shd;aul ù wjika jQ miq end=" " u.ska b;=re m%;sodko tu fma,sfhau we;s ùu je,elaùug
    fhdod we;' fuu.ska ysia fma<shla m%;sodkh lrkq ,nhs' kuq;a fuys óg fmr m%;sodkfha end=" " f,i
    mej;s neúka tu ysia fma<sh tu fma<sfhau m%;sodkh fjhs' kuq;a fuys end=" " f,i fkdue;s neúka
    ó,. m%;sodkh ó,. fma<shg .uka lrhs'

                      2024 AL PYTHON MCQ MARKING

   1. What will be the output of the following Python code if a = 5, b = 3, c = 2 and d = 6?
      my; mhs;ka fla;fha a = 5, b = 3, c = 2 iy d = 6 kï tys l%shd;aul m%;sodkh l=ula fõo@
          x = ( a – b ) ** c + d % c
          print(x)

          1) -22                           2) 0                             3) 1                             4) 4                             5) 7
                                                                                                                                                         ( 2024 - 38 )
          Answer : 4 )

          Given values / ,nd § we;s w.hka:
          •     a=5
          •     b=3
          •     c=2
          •     d=6

          Expression / m%ldYKh:
          x = (a–b)**c + d % c

          Compute / .Kkh lsÍu(
          •     a–b
                a−b = 5−3 = 2

          •     (a−b)**c
                (a−b)**c = 22 = 4

          •     d % c
                The modulus division (%) gives the remainder of the division of d by c:
                udmdxl fn§u ] uÕska d ys c j,ska fn§fï b;sßh ,nd fohs(
                d % c = 6 % 2 = 0

          Add the results / m%;sM, tl;= lsÍu
          x = (a−b)**c + d % c
          x = 4 + 0
          x = 4

          Output / m%;sodkh:
          4

          Therefore, the answer is 4).
          tneúka" ms<s;=r 4) fõ'

 2. What is the execution output of the following Python code?
    my; mhs;ka fla;fha l%shd;aul m%;sodkh l=ula fõo@
    qns = ["a" , "b"]
    for x in range (1,3):
        for y in qns:
            print(x,y, end=" ")

    1) 0 a 2 b                       2) 1 a 3 b                       3) 1 a 1 b 2 a 2 b
    4) 1 a 1 b 3 a 3 b               5) 1 a 3 a 1 b 3 b

                                                                                               ( 2024 - 39 )
    Answer : 3 )

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu:

    The outer loop iterates over values of x generated by range(1, 3), i.e. x = 1 and x = 2.
    msg; Æmh range(1, 3) u.ska ckkh lrk ,o x w.hka u; mqkrdj¾;kh fõ" tkï, x = 1 iy x = 2'

 3. What is the execution output of the following Python code?
    my; mhs;ka fla;fha l%shd;aul m%;sodkh l=ula fõo@
    def list_operation(nlist):
        for i in range(len(nlist)):
            if i % 2 ==0:
                nlist[i] = nlist[i] ** 2
            else:
                nlist[i] = nlist[i] + 3
        return nlist
    numbers=[1,2,3,4,5]
    output = list_operation(numbers)
    print(output)

    1) [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]                   2) [1, 5, 9, 7, 25]             3) [2, 5, 6, 7, 10]
    4) [4, 4, 6, 16, 8]                  5) [4, 6,16, 8, 36]
                                                                                                      ( 2024 - 40 )
    Answer : 2 )
    Input List :
    numbers = [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]

    Function Logic / ld¾hh ;¾lkh:
    •    A for loop iterates over the indices (i) of the list using range(len(nlist)).
         range(len(nlist)) Ndú;d lrñka ,ehsia;=fõ o¾Yl (i) u; mqkrdj¾;kh fõ'
    •    For each element / tla tla uQ,øjH i|yd:
         −       If the index i is even (i % 2 == 0), the element at that index is squared:
                 i o¾Ylh brÜfÜ kï (i % 2 == 0) tu o¾Ylfha we;s uQ,øjHh j¾. lr we;(
                 nlist[i] = nlist[i] **2.
         −       If the index i is odd (i % 2 != 0), the element at that index is incremented by 3:
                 i o¾Ylh T;af;a kï (i % 2 != 0)" tu o¾Ylfha uQ,øjHh 3 lska jeä fõ(
                 nlist[i] = nlist[i] + 3.

    Detailed Iteration / iúia;rd;aul mqkrdj¾;kh:

        Index (i)         Element (nlist[i])              Condition              Updated Value
             0                      1                Even (0 % 2 = 0)                12 = 1
             1                      2                 Odd (1 % 2 = 1)              2 + 3 = 5

    [1, 5, 9, 7, 25]

 5. which of the following statements are true about the web pages created using web authoring tools?

    C - Multimedia content cannot be added to them.

    Explanation of each statement / tla tla m%ldYh meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    Statement / m%ldYh A
    This is true. Web authoring tools like Adobe Dreamweaver or WordPress generate HTML code in the
    background automatically as users design the webpage visually. Users don't need to write the HTML
    manually unless they want to customize further.
    fuh ksjerÈ fõ' Adobe Dreamweaver fyda WordPress jeks fjí l¾;D fuj,ï mßYS,lhka úiska

    Statement / m%ldYh B

    the generated HTML code. They can manually add HTML tags to improve or customize the page.

    ,o HTML fla;h ne,Sug iy fjkia lsÍug úl,amhla we;' msgqj jeäÈhqKq lsÍug fyda wNsreÑlrKh
    lsÍug Tjqkag w;ska HTML ge.a tla l< yel'

    Statement / m%ldYh C
    This is false. Multimedia content, such as images, audio, video, and animations, can be easily added to

    multimedia elements seamlessly.
